## Examining the Scriptures Daily

### _Foreword_

“How I do love your law! All day long it is my concern.” (Ps. 119:97) So said the ancient psalmist, and surely God’s people today agree with his words! What a valuable treasure we have in the Bible! It is truly _God’s_ Word. When we read it, we read thoughts that our Creator himself inspired and caused to be written. And today that Word is even more extensive than the Law that the psalmist so much appreciated.

The Bible helps us to live our lives according to God’s will. As ‘a lamp to our foot and a light to our roadway,’ it is “beneficial for teaching, for reproving, for setting things straight, for disciplining in righteousness.” (Ps. 119:105; 2 Tim. 3:16) Further, the Bible warns us of the traps and pitfalls that Satan places in our way. And it gives comfort to the depressed and solace to those who are suffering.

Most important, the Bible helps us to build and maintain a close, personal relationship with Jehovah God. When anointed Christians read in the Bible about their glorious heavenly hope, they know that this is the spirit of their heavenly Father speaking directly to them, reinforcing their faith. When those of the “other sheep” read the magnificent promises of the coming Paradise earth, they have no doubt that this is God’s portion for them. (John 10:16) They accept God’s words with total confidence.—Matt. 25:34; Rom. 8:16.

However, loving God’s Word means more than merely having the Bible in our home. Many members of Christendom’s religions possess a Bible. But how many read it regularly and apply its life-giving words to their lives? Christians, though, never forget Jesus’ words: “Man must live, not on bread alone, but on every utterance coming forth through Jehovah’s mouth.” (Matt. 4:4) They recognize that a regular, yes, daily, intake of spiritual food is as essential to their well-being as regularly eating material food.

Further, they are aware of Jesus’ words about his disciples when he said: “They are no part of the world, just as I am no part of the world.” (John 17:16) Why must a Christian be no part of this world? Because the world lies in the power of Satan, and the spirit of the world is the very antithesis of godliness and spirituality. (Eph. 2:2; 1 John 5:19) Nevertheless, it is difficult not to be affected by the world. Earning a

---

<sup>**Page 4**</sup>

[Open Page 4 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1992_es_E.pdf#page=4)

living can be an ordeal that uses up much of our physical strength. Different types of entertainment can gradually steal our time and our spirituality. Unwholesome companionship can erode our spiritual strength.

Such is the pressure of the world that if we do not take a positive stand against it, we can easily drift away from our wholesome relationship with God. How can we prevent that? Since we face these pressures each day, we should take in spiritual nourishment daily to be strengthened to resist them.

In harmony with this, the psalmist wrote: 'Happy is the man . . . \[whose] delight is in the law of Jehovah, and in his law he reads in an undertone day and night. And he will certainly become like a tree planted by streams of water, that gives its own fruit in its season and the foliage of which does not wither, and everything he does will succeed.'—Ps. 1:1-3.

Thus, the faithful and discreet slave class, as God's steward, encourages all to have a regular Bible reading program. And through congregation meetings and Bible-based publications, it provides a constant supply of Scriptural counsel to counteract the influence of the world. Throughout the time of the end, the _Watchtower_ magazine has been a prominent instrument in providing such spiritual food, and the publication _Examining the Scriptures Daily_ is an excellent aid in getting even more out of timely _Watchtower_ Studies that were enjoyed in the recent past.

Each day, a key scripture is quoted and a comment on this scripture from a past issue of _The Watchtower_ is added. Reading the text and comment for each day is a fine stimulus to meditate on the Scriptures daily. And further study can be enjoyed if one reads the context of the scripture and looks up the _Watchtower_ study article that the comment is taken from.

In all the Bethel families around the world, the day's text from _Examining the Scriptures_ is discussed in a session of morning worship before breakfast. Can you follow that pattern? If possible, why not get together with your family and read the text and comment together at breakfast or at some other time. If you cannot do this as a family, at least be sure to have a regular schedule as an individual to read the day's text and comment. In that way, God's Word will be your concern all day long. Your faith will be regularly reinforced, and you will be strengthened to keep walking along that narrow path that leads to eternal life.—Matt. 7:13, 14.

## 1992 Yeartext

### 'Rejoice in the hope . . . Persevere in prayer.'—Rom. 12:12.

As Jehovah's Witnesses, our Bible-based hope is indeed something over which to rejoice greatly. As we stand at the threshold of another year, it is good for us to contemplate all that is embodied in our wonderful, God-given hope.

Above all else, we hope for the early vindication of Jehovah's sovereignty and the sanctification of his holy name. Hence, we daily pray: 'Let your name be sanctified.' (Matt. 6:9) And to think that we can individually share in clearing Jehovah's name of the reproach cast upon it! That we can do by our faithfully enduring to the end of this system of things. What an honor! Not only that, but our faithful endurance will lead to our eventual salvation to life in God's righteous new world.—Matt. 24:13.

Life in God's new world—can you imagine what that will mean? For the remaining members of the anointed, it will mean living in a heavenly home and sharing for a thousand years with the King, Christ Jesus, in dispensing Kingdom blessings to earth's inhabitants. For the millions of Jesus' companionable 'other sheep,' life in God's new world means inheriting an earthly paradise where beauty, peace, and endless pleasures will be truly beyond description. (John 10:16; compare 1 Corinthians 2:10.) Add to this the joy of welcoming back the resurrected millions who are still in God's memory.—John 5:28.

Who apart from us has such a marvelous hope? No one. How grateful we should be! Nevertheless, from time to time, the pressures of Satan's old system do seem overwhelming. Quite often, too, in one way or another, this world's calamities and tragedies affect the lives of God's people. When this happens, it is good immediately to cause our mind to dwell upon the wonderful aspects of our God-given hope. Let it become like 'a tree of life' that lives on within our heart. Let it be like 'an anchor for our soul' that keeps us standing firm in the truth. Then we will experience the reality of Peter's encouraging words: 'God . . . will make you firm, he will make you strong.'—Prov. 13:12; Heb. 6:19; 1 Pet. 5:10.

Jesus set us the perfect example to follow in this regard. Think of all that he had to put up with and endure. Yet he was confidently able to say: 'Take courage! I have conquered the

---

<sup>**Page 5**</sup>

[Open Page 5 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1992_es_E.pdf#page=5)

world.' (John 16:33) What gave Jesus such courage? Among other things, it was the joyful hope that he had set before him, as Paul explains at Hebrews 12:1-3. Also, Jesus used prayer to keep that hope ever bright before him. He taught his disciples to do the same. Jesus prayed under all kinds of circumstances and at all times of the day and night. He persevered in talking to his Father. Do we do the same, as Paul advised: 'Persevere in prayer'?

We need to talk to our heavenly Father regularly. We may not take God's goodness for granted. Daily we have to ask God for what we need and then, before the day ends, express appreciation for what he has given us.—Jas. 1:17.

If we truly appreciate the privilege of talking to the greatest Person in the universe, we will not limit our prayers to regular occasions, such as when we first awaken in the morning, before we eat, and the last thing at night before going to sleep. No, with gladness we will spontaneously speak to Jehovah as various situations arise during the day. If we do this, it will prove that we are taking Jehovah into consideration in everything we do. (1 Cor. 10:31) Imagine how this will draw us ever closer to our heavenly Father!—Jas. 4:8.

However, there is another aspect of our persevering in prayer. Sometimes it may appear that we do not get an answer to a prayer. Do we hastily conclude that Jehovah's answer is no and therefore feel that it is useless to ask again? If we feel sure that what we are asking for is in harmony with Jehovah's will, why not persevere in our request but, of course, adding, 'If it be your will, Father'? Jehovah may be testing our sincerity, as illustrated in Jesus' example of the widow and the judge, where he showed 'the need for his disciples always to pray and not give up.'—Luke 18:1-8.

During the coming year, may all of us, then, continue to rejoice in our marvelous hope, drawing ever nearer to our heavenly Father by constant prayer, because we know for a certainty that our hope 'does not lead to disappointment.'—Rom. 5:5.

## DAILY TEXTS AND COMMENTS

There is a text for each day and a comment on that text. The comments are taken from the _Watchtower_ (w) issues of 1990/91. Figures following the date of the _Watchtower_ issue refer to paragraphs in the first study article, where further comment on the text may be found. When 'a' follows the paragraph number, the comment is found in the second study article; when 'b' is shown, it refers to the third study article.

## Wednesday, January 1

_That statement is faithful. If any man is reaching out for an office of overseer, he is desirous of a fine work.—1 Tim. 3:1._

Jehovah's Witnesses have proper objectives that are directed and carried out in a godly way. This is not surprising, since their God has noble objectives and always accomplishes his purposes. (Isa. 55: 8-11) Jehovah's servants should not be like those people who lack a fine objective and pass heedlessly through life doing little to benefit anyone apart from themselves. Of prime importance to God's Witnesses is the fulfilling of the noble objective of proclaiming the Kingdom message and sharing with others the life-giving knowledge of God's Word. (Ps. 119:105; Mark 13:10; John 17:3) In Jehovah's organization, there are also other noble objectives. The apostle Paul cited one of these when he wrote the above. Such a man wants to accomplish something for the good of others. He desires 'a fine work,' not a life of ease and glory. w 9/1/90 1, 2

## Thursday, January 2

_I will exalt you, O my God the King, and I will bless your name to time indefinite, even forever.—Ps. 145:1._

With similar reverence, Jehovah's Witnesses lift up the name of God and praise him earth wide. Our life is enriched as we share in such activity. Like David, we owe obedience and subjection to Jehovah as 'our God the King.' David said that he would 'bless God's name.' How is this possible for a mere human? Well, among other things, to bless someone means to speak well of him. Blessing God's

name indicates that we have strong love for him and his holy name, Jehovah. We never complain about God, never find fault with him, never question his goodness. Only if we have that attitude, have made a dedication to Jehovah, and maintain integrity as his baptized Witnesses can we say with David that we 'will bless God's name forever.' If we keep ourselves in God's love, we will receive the gift of everlasting life and thus be able to bless Jehovah eternally. w 9/15/90 3, 4

## Friday, January 3

_Do not toil to gain riches. Cease from your own understanding. Have you caused your eyes to glance at it, when it is nothing? For without fail it makes wings for itself like those of an eagle and flies away toward the heavens.—Prov. 23:4, 5._

Jehovah is interested in your devotion to him, not in your bank account. He knows that your heart is where your treasure is. Can rust and moths consume your treasure? Can thieves dig through mud walls and steal it? Or in these modern times of economic instability, can inflation shrink its buying power or can stock-market crashes wipe it out? Will the soaring crime rate cause your treasure to be stolen? Not if it is stored in heaven. Not if your eye—a lamp that lights up your whole body—is simple, focused on God's Kingdom and his righteousness. Riches have a way of disappearing. So why lose sleep over wealth? 'The plenty belonging to the rich one is not permitting him to sleep.' (Eccl. 5:12) Remember Jesus' warning: 'You cannot slave for God and for Riches.'—Matt. 6: 19-24. w 10/1/90 9a

## Saturday, January 4

_This good news of the kingdom will be preached in all the inhabited earth for a witness to all the nations; and then the end will come.—Matt. 24:14._

Ever since 1919, faithful anointed Christians as a group have zealously obeyed the command involved in Jesus' words above. As a result, over four million of Christ's 'other sheep' have devoted their lives to serving Jehovah in unity with the anointed remnant. (John 10:16) Christendom, under the influence of its clergy, continues to reject the Kingdom message. Its preference for human political schemes and its persecution of Jehovah's Witnesses parallel the shocking treatment of Christ by the first-century inhabitants of Judea. Just as Jehovah executed his judgment upon Jerusalem, so he will do to antitypical unfaithful Jerusalem, namely, Christendom. And as the generation that heard Christ's judgment message lived to experience the destruction he foretold, so the present generation since 1914 'will by no means pass away' before the foretold 'great tribulation' comes.—Matt. 24:21, 22, 34. _w 10/15/90_ 23a

## Sunday, January 5

_Let us consider one another to incite to love and fine works, not forsaking the gathering of ourselves together, as some have the custom.—Heb. 10:24, 25._

If congregation meetings are banned, Christians will find some less obvious way to continue feeding at Jehovah's table. The Supreme Authority, Jehovah God, tells us the above through Paul. But such gatherings can be held discreetly. Even if just a few are present, we can be confident that

God blesses such arrangements. Similarly, some authorities have forbidden the public preaching of the good news. Christians living under them remember that, through Jesus himself, the Supreme Authority said: 'In all the nations the good news has to be preached first.' (Mark 13:10) Hence, they obey the Supreme Authority at whatever cost to themselves. Where possible, the apostles preached publicly and from house to house, but there are other ways of reaching people, such as informal witnessing. _w 11/1/90_ 19, 20b

## Monday, January 6

_Do not you people be owing anybody a single thing, except to love one another; for he that loves his fellowman has fulfilled the law. For the law code . . . is summed up in this word, namely, 'You must love your neighbor as yourself.' Love does not work evil to one's neighbor; therefore love is the law's fulfillment.—Rom. 13:8-10._

Supporting the conclusion that love is the greatest of the fruits of God's spirit are Paul's words above. Fittingly, the disciple James refers to this law of loving one's neighbor as oneself as 'the kingly law.' (Jas. 2:8) That love is the greatest quality is borne out by what Jesus said were the two greatest commandments, as recorded at Mark 12:28-31. Truly, Paul did not exaggerate when he mentioned faith, hope, and love and said: 'The greatest of these is love.' (1 Cor. 13:13) The displaying of love results in good relations with our heavenly Father and others, including those in the congregation and members of our families. Love has an upbuilding effect on us. _w 11/15/90_ 17-19

## Tuesday, January 7

_The minding of the flesh means death, but the minding of the spirit means life and peace.—Rom. 8:6._

Peace is part of the fruitage of the spirit, and if we train our heart to appreciate spiritual things, praying for God's spirit to help us in this, then we will avoid 'the minding of the flesh.' (Gal. 5:22-24) At 1 Peter 3:10-12, peace is linked with righteousness. (Rom. 5:1) Peter says that the pursuing of peace includes 'turning away from what is bad and doing what is good.' God's spirit can help us to 'pursue righteousness' and thus preserve our peace with God. (1 Tim. 6:11, 12) The pursuit of peace is of major concern to the elders in the congregation. For example, if someone tries to introduce polluting practices, the elders are responsible to protect the congregation by trying to reprove the sinner. If he accepts the reproof, he will regain his peace. (Heb. 12:11) If not, he may have to be cast out so that the peaceful relationship of the congregation with Jehovah may be preserved. _w 3/1/91_ 5, 6a

## Wednesday, January 8

_Roll upon Jehovah your way, and rely upon him, and he himself will act. A young man I used to be, I have also grown old, and yet I have not seen anyone righteous left entirely, nor his offspring looking for bread.—Ps. 37:5, 25._

In our daily lives, many situations may call for endurance. Physical sickness, depression, and economic hardship are just a few. How can the Christian maintain his joy in order to cope with such trials? This can be done by going

to God's Word for comfort and guidance. Reading or listening to readings of the psalms can provide much refreshment in time of trial. And note David's wise advice: 'Throw your burden upon Jehovah himself, and he himself will sustain you. Never will he allow the righteous one to totter.' (Ps. 55:22) Jehovah is indeed the 'Hearer of prayer.' (Ps. 65:2) Jehovah's organization, through its publications and its congregation elders, stands ever at the ready to help us, frail humans that we are, to grapple with our problems. David warmly counsels as above. _w 1/1/91_ 18, 19

## Thursday, January 9

_Trust in Jehovah with all your heart and do not lean upon your own understanding.—Prov. 3:5._

In view of all of Jehovah's qualities, how can we honor him? We can honor him by showing him fear and reverence, by acknowledging him in all our ways, by making gifts, by imitating him, and by making petitions to him. We can also honor him by putting faith in him, by trusting him no matter what happens. A closely related way that we honor Jehovah God is by obeying him. And vital to obedience is godly fear, yes, fear of displeasing God. Showing the relationship between fearing and obeying are Jehovah's words to Abraham after Abraham obediently attempted to sacrifice Isaac, his son. 'Now I do know that you are God-fearing,' Jehovah said. (Gen. 22:12) When discussing what children owe their parents, the apostle Paul also showed that obedience and honor go hand in hand. (Eph. 6:1-3) So by obeying God's commandments, which are not burdensome, we honor Jehovah.—1 John 5:3. _w 2/1/91_ 11, 12

## Friday, January 10

_Jesus . . . suffered death, that he by God's undeserved kindness might taste death for every man.—Heb. 2:9._

Neither Adam nor Eve, however, benefit from the ransom. The Mosaic Law contained this principle: 'You must take no ransom for the soul of a murderer who is deserving to die.' (Num. 35:31) Adam was not deceived, so his sin was willful, deliberate. (1 Tim. 2:14) It amounted to the murder of his offspring, for they now inherited his imperfection, thus coming under sentence of death. Clearly, Adam deserved to die, for as a perfect man, he had willfully chosen to disobey God's law. It would have been contrary to Jehovah's righteous principles for him to apply the ransom in Adam's behalf. Paying the wage for Adam's sin, however, does provide for the nullifying of the death sentence upon Adam's offspring! (Rom. 5:16) In a legal sense, the destructive power of sin is cut off right at its source. The ransomer 'tastes death for every man,' bearing the consequences of sin for all of Adam's children.—2 Cor. 5:21; 1 Pet. 2:24. _w 2/15/91_ 13

## Saturday, January 11

_Go therefore and make disciples of people of all the nations, baptising them in the name of the Father and of the Son and of the holy spirit.—Matt. 28:19._

True Christians today find it appropriate to identify the One of whom they are the foretold Witnesses, even as Jesus Christ their Leader did when he was here on earth. (Rev. 1:1, 2) Yes, they identify him alone as the one whose name is Jehovah. (Ps. 83:18) Jesus Christ said the above in his part-

ing words to his apostles. Note that newly taught Christians were to be baptized in the name of the holy spirit. This holy spirit is the active force of Jehovah God. At Pentecost, Jehovah God, through Jesus, poured out this active force upon the dedicated followers of Jesus Christ. (Acts 2:33) They were filled with this holy spirit, and one of the expressions, or fruits, of the holy spirit is joy. (Gal. 5:22, 23; Eph. 5:18-20) Joy is a stimulating quality. The disciples are to be filled with the joy of the holy spirit. _w 12/15/90_ 9-11a

## Sunday, January 12

_They came in greater numbers to \[Paul] in his lodging place. And he explained the matter to them by bearing thorough witness concerning the kingdom of God and by using persuasion with them.—Acts 28:23._

Persuasion has a proper place in the Christian ministry. For instance, Paul urged his coworker Timothy to continue in the things he had learned and was 'persuaded to believe.' (2 Tim. 3:14) In Corinth, Paul 'would give a talk in the synagogue every sabbath and would persuade Jews and Greeks.' (Acts 18:1-4) In Ephesus, he successfully 'gave talks and used persuasion concerning the kingdom of God.' (Acts 19:8) And when under house arrest in Rome, the apostle called people to him and gave them a witness, 'using persuasion,' even as noted above. Regardless of how persuasive the Witness may try to be, of course, only those rightly disposed for everlasting life will become believers. Convincing arguments and clear explanations, tactfully presented, may persuade them to believe. _w 1/15/91_ 12, 13a

## Monday, January 13

_He did good, giving you rains from heaven and fruitful seasons, filling your hearts to the full with food and good cheer.—Acts 14:17._

'God is love.' How full of meaning those words are! No wonder the apostle John felt it necessary to repeat them in his first letter. (1 John 4:8, 16) Not only is Jehovah God love but he is also the embodiment, or personification, of love. Think of the love God displayed in the way he created us. David's appreciative words are so fitting. He said: 'I shall laud you because in a fear-inspiring way I am wonderfully made.' (Ps. 139:14) God made endless delights possible for us by means of our five senses—seeing, hearing, tasting, smelling, and feeling. What beauty we see in creation all around us! How wonderful are the many and varied plants and animals, to say nothing of the beauties of human forms and features! God also put a most delightful array of fruits, vegetables, and other foods within our reach. (Ps. 104:13-16) For good reason, the apostle Paul said the above. _w 12/1/90_ 1, 2

## Tuesday, January 14

_The appearance of the wheels and their structure . . . were just as when a wheel proved to be in the midst of a wheel.—Ezek. 1:16._

What is pictured by this marvelous chariot? The celestial, or heavenly, organization of Jehovah God. It is composed of all of his holy spirit creatures in the invisible realm—seraphs, cherubs, and angels. Since Jehovah is the Most High God, all of his spirit creatures are subject to him, and he rides them in the sense of benevolently

lently dominating them and using them according to his purpose. Jehovah rides this organization as if on a chariot, causing it to move to wherever his spirit impels it to move. It follows his directions. Together, all are moving unitedly forward to the full accomplishment of God's objectives. What a marvelous heavenly organization is revealed by this vision of Jehovah's four-wheeled celestial chariot on the move! In harmony with this, Jehovah's organization is represented as being foursquare, in perfect balance. _w 3/15/91_ 5, 12, 13

## Wednesday, January 15

_Turn back at my reproof. Then to you I will cause my spirit to bubble forth; I will make my words known to you.—Prov. 1:23._

We want to encourage all we can to become happy, active Witnesses of Jehovah in the final phase of the great work now being accomplished worldwide. Please note Jehovah's loving invitation above. How encouraging to know that Jehovah will respond to our taking positive steps to identify ourselves with his name and worship! Certainly, we do not want to be included among those described at Proverbs 1:24, 25: 'I have called out but you keep refusing, I have stretched out my hand but there is no one paying attention, and you keep neglecting all my counsel, and my reproof you have not accepted.' Those who neglect Jehovah's counsel that they search for him while he may be found and who put off their decision until they actually see the great tribulation starting will find they have waited too long. Such a course would indicate a lack of faith and wisdom and would be contemptuous of Jehovah's undeserved kindness. _w 4/1/91_ 8, 9

## Thursday, January 16

_That statement is faithful. If any man is reaching out for an office of overseer, he is desirous of a fine work. The overseer should therefore be irreprehensible.—1 Tim. 3:1, 2._

When appointed and while serving, ministerial servants and elders must be irreprehensible, that is, free of blame and of any need to be reproved for a _just_ accusation of wrong conduct or teaching. (1 Tim. 3:7, 8, 10; Titus 1:6, 7) _Untrue_ charges made by 'false brothers' or others do not make a man reprehensible. To disqualify a man from serving in the congregation, a charge must not be frivolous, and it must be proved in harmony with Scriptural standards. (2 Cor. 11:26; 1 Tim. 5:19) One appointed in the congregation 'should also have a fine testimony from people on the outside, in order that he might not fall into reproach and a snare of the Devil.' _w 9/1/90 5a_

## Friday, January 17

_You heard that it was said, 'You must not commit adultery.' But I say to you that everyone that keeps on looking at a woman so as to have a passion for her has already committed adultery with her in his heart.—Matt. 5:27, 28._

You are not going to commit adultery? Then do not even start down that road by entertaining thoughts about it. Guard your heart, where such things have their source. (Prov. 4:23; Matt. 15:18, 19) James 1:14, 15 warns: 'Each one is tried by being drawn

out and enticed by his own desire. Then the desire, when it has become fertile, gives birth to sin; in turn, sin, when it has been accomplished, brings forth death.' People sometimes say: 'Don't start what you can't finish.' But in this case we should say: 'Don't start what you can't stop.' Some who have been faithful even when threatened with death before a firing squad have later fallen for the insidious lure of sexual immorality. _w 10/1/90 14_

## Saturday, January 18

_The glorious splendor of your dignity and the matters of your wonderful works I will make my concern.—Ps. 145:5._

As we reflect on God's works and mighty acts, we become even more convinced that 'Jehovah will not desert his people for the sake of his great name.' (1 Sam. 12:22; Ps. 94:14) When we face trials, hardships, and persecution, we can be tranquil and confident that 'the peace of God' will guard our hearts and mental powers. (Phil. 4:6, 7) So how fitting it is that we tell others about our loving, protective heavenly Father! God's dignity is fear-inspiring and unequaled. (Job 37:22; Ps. 148:13) Thus, David made the glorious splendor of Jehovah's dignity his concern. The psalmist also concerned himself with matters of God's 'wonderful works.' These include the exercise of divine justice in destroying sinners and preserving the godly, as at the Flood. Meditation on such matters strengthens our relationship with Jehovah and enables us to tell others about his dignity and wonderful works. _w 9/15/90 9, 10_

## Sunday, January 19

_Be wise, my son, and make my heart rejoice, that I may make a reply to him that is taunting me.—Prov. 27:11._

Because of God's almighty power, we dare not arouse him to jealousy. Paul asked: 'Are we inciting Jehovah to jealousy? We are not stronger than he is, are we?' (1 Cor. 10:22) Of course, Jehovah is 'a jealous God,' not in a bad sense, but in 'exacting exclusive devotion.' (Ex. 20:5; _King James Version_) As Christians, we are awestruck at the many manifestations of God's unfathomable wisdom. (Rom. 11:33-35) Our great respect for his justice should cause us to keep far away from willful sin. (Heb. 10:26-31) But love unquestionably is the greatest of God's four principal attributes. And it is Jehovah's unselfish love that draws us to him and makes us want to please him, to worship him, and to share in the sanctification of his holy name. _w 11/15/90 12_

## Monday, January 20

_Let every soul be in subjection to the superior authorities, for there is no authority except by God; the existing authorities stand placed in their relative positions by God.—Rom. 13:1._

In calling the secular authorities 'superior,' do we somehow detract from the honor due Jehovah? No, because Jehovah is much more than merely superior. He is 'the Sovereign Lord,' 'the Supreme One.' (Ps. 73:28; Dan. 7:18, 22, 25, 27; Rev. 4:11; 6:10) In no way does a proper subjection to human authorities detract from our worship of the Supreme Au-

Authority, the Sovereign Lord Jehovah. To what extent, then, are these authorities superior? Merely with regard to other humans and in their own sphere of activity. They are responsible to govern and protect human communities, and for this they lay down regulations as to the conduct of public affairs. Jehovah God's supremacy over even the secular authorities is seen in that these authorities 'stand placed in their relative positions by God.' _w 11/1/90 9, 10_

## Tuesday, January 21

_The man Moses was by far the meekest of all the men who were upon the surface of the ground.—Num. 12:3._

At one time, Moses wanted to deliver his brothers, the Israelites, from Egyptian bondage. (Acts 7:23-29) Decades later, however, because of Moses' faith and unselfish desire to help his brothers, Jehovah empowered him to perform many miracles and to serve the Israelites for 40 years as His prophet, judge, lawgiver, and mediator. In these capacities, Moses experienced many instances of Jehovah's love for him and his fellow Israelites. How did Moses respond to God's love and undeserved kindness? Did he 'accept Jehovah's undeserved kindness and miss its purpose?' (2 Cor. 6:1) By no means! Moses unselfishly responded to Jehovah's expressions of love toward him by being wholly God-oriented. He looked to Jehovah at all times and had an intimate relationship with his Maker. In spite of Moses' many privileges, he continued to be the meekest of men and carried out Jehovah's commands 'just so.'—Ex. 40:16. _w 12/1/90 6, 7_

## Wednesday, January 22

_“To whom can you people liken me so that I should be made his equal?” says the Holy One._

The Greek word translated “honor” in the Bible is _ti-me_, and it conveys the sense of esteem, value, preciousness. Thus one honors another by showing that person deep respect and esteem. Who above all others is deserving of honor? Why, Jehovah God, of course! He is deserving of our honor because he positively is grand. Often the term “grand” is applied to him. He is the Grand One, the Grand Maker, the Grand Creator, the Grand King, the Grand Instructor, the Grand Master. (Ps. 48:2; Eccl. 12:1; Isa. 30:20; 42:5; 54:5; Hos. 12:14) He who is grand is majestic, dignified, elevated, noble, illustrious, and awe inspiring. Jehovah is beyond comparison, he has no equal, he is transcendent. He himself testifies to that fact, saying: “To whom will you people liken me or make me equal or compare me that we may resemble each other?”—Isa. 46:5. _w 2/1/91_ 3, 4, 6

## Thursday, January 23

_The Son of man came, not to be ministered to, but to minister and to give his soul a ransom in exchange for many._

The Bible helps us understand matters by using a number of terms that describe or illustrate God’s grand works from a variety of viewpoints. The Bible speaks of the ransom in terms of a purchase, a reconciliation, a propitiation, a redemption, and an atonement. But perhaps the expression that best describes matters was the one

used by Jesus himself, as noted above. What is a ransom? The Greek word _ly’tron_ comes from a verb meaning “to loose.” It was used to describe money paid in exchange for the releasing of prisoners of war. In the Hebrew Scriptures, however, the word for ransom, _ko’pher_, comes from a verb meaning to “cover” or “overlay.” For example, God told Noah to cover (_ka-phar_) the ark with tar. (Gen. 6:14) From this viewpoint, then, to ransom, or to atone for sins, means to cover sins.—Ps. 65:3. _w 2/15/91_ 6, 7

## Friday, January 24

_For if, when we were enemies, we became reconciled to God through the death of his Son, much more, now that we have become reconciled, we shall be saved by his life._—Rom. 5:10.

The Seed that was to restore complete peace arrived in the person of Jesus, and at his birth angels sang: “Glory in the heights above to God, and upon earth peace among men of goodwill.” (Luke 2:14) Jesus appeared in Israel, but despite being under God’s covenant, that nation as a whole rejected him and turned him over to the Romans to be killed. Because of rejecting Jesus, Israel completely lost its peace with God. Nevertheless, God’s purposes were not thwarted. Jesus was resurrected from the dead, and he offered to Jehovah the value of his perfect life as a ransom for righthearted humans. (Heb. 9:11-14) Jesus’ sacrifice became a new and better way for humans—both natural Israelites and Gentiles—to find peace with God. Paul in his letter to the Christians in Rome said the above. _w 3/1/91_ 12, 13

## Saturday, January 25

_The spirit and the bride keep on saying: “Come!” And let anyone hearing say: “Come!”_ —Rev. 22:17.

The first to extend the invitation to “come!” are those who make up the figurative bride of the Lamb, the firstborn spiritual Son of Jehovah. The spiritual bride of Christ is not saying, “Come!” to herself, that is, to those whom Jehovah God would yet gather as part of the bride class in order to make it 144,000 strong. Those words of invitation are extended to humans who hope to attain to perfect human life on earth after Armageddon. During this conclusion of the system of things since 1914, we have heard the invitation being extended by “the bride,” in collaboration with the holy spirit of God. Thrillingly, the last book of the Bible shows that “a great crowd, whom no man could number,” would respond to the proclamation of the good news of God’s Kingdom and would firmly put themselves on the side of that royal government. (Rev. 7:9, 10, 16, 17) Are you one of that great crowd? Then, “let anyone hearing say: ‘Come!’” _w 12/15/90_ 4, 8, 9

## Sunday, January 26

_As regards Jehovah, his eyes are roving about through all the earth to show his strength in behalf of those whose heart is complete toward him._ —2 Chron. 16:9.

The vision of God’s celestial chariot helps us to appreciate that we should move in harmony with God’s organization, visible and invisible. (Ezek., chap. 1) Keep in mind, too, the above words. Not a single thing escapes Jehovah’s no-

notice, especially anything regarding his purpose to vindicate himself as Universal Sovereign. Jehovah’s celestial chariot is certainly on the move today. Soon everything will be brought to glory in harmony with the glorious One who rides that chariot—all in vindication of him as the Sovereign Lord of the universe. His seraphs, cherubs, and angels are backing us up in our great worldwide preaching work. Let us move ahead, then, with Jehovah’s heavenly organization. But how can we keep pace with that fast-moving celestial chariot? By personal study, meeting attendance, prayer, and witnessing. _w 3/15/91_ 22, 23

## Monday, January 27

_Everyone that is proud in heart is something detestable to Jehovah. Hand may join to hand, yet one will not be free from punishment._—Prov. 16:5.

An elder should not lord it over other Christians, for he is their fellow worker, not “a master over their faith.” (2 Cor. 1:24) When certain apostles sought prominence, Jesus said: “You know that the rulers of the nations lord it over them and the great men wield authority over them. This is not the way among you; but whoever wants to become great among you must be your minister, and whoever wants to be first among you must be your slave. Just as the Son of man came, not to be ministered to, but to minister and to give his soul a ransom in exchange for many.” (Matt. 20:20-28) An elder is not the Chief Shepherd but only an undershepherd. If he lords it over the flock, he manifests a spirit of pride. Especially would harm result if he enticed others to help him further his proud ambitions, as emphasized above. _w 9/1/90_ 6

## Tuesday, January 28

_Jehovah is great and very much to be praised, and his greatness is unsearchable.—Ps. 145:3._

So great is Jehovah that he is matchless, and his sovereignty is absolute. Babylonian king Nebuchadnezzar had to admit: 'There exists no one that can check \[God's] hand or that can say to him, 'What have you been doing?'' (Dan. 4:34, 35) Jehovah 'is fear-inspiring above all other gods.' (Ps. 96:4) No wonder he is 'very much to be praised.' Why, no words of praise are too lofty when lauding Jehovah! He deserves unlimited, eternal praise. Jehovah's 'greatness is unsearchable.' Regardless of how large he is bodily, his greatness rests in the kind of God he is. Yes, the things he has created are too wonderful for us to understand, and we ourselves are 'wonderfully made.' (Ps. 139:14; Job 9:10; 37:5) Moreover, how grandly Jehovah God conducts himself! He faithfully keeps his promises and lovingly reveals his purposes. Yet, we will never know everything about God. _w 9/15/90_ 6, 7

## Wednesday, January 29

_Happy are those conscious of their spiritual need, since the kingdom of the heavens belongs to them.—Matt. 5:3._

In the opening sentence of his Sermon on the Mount, Jesus spoke of the Kingdom of the heavens as belonging to those conscious of their spiritual need. In the fourth sentence, he said that those hungering and thirsting for righteousness would be filled. Here Jesus puts both the Kingdom and Jehovah's righteousness in first place. They are the theme of the Sermon

on the Mount. They are the answer to the needs of all mankind. But by what means do God's Kingdom and God's righteousness become attainable? How do we continue seeking them? Not the way the scribes and Pharisees did. They sought the Kingdom and righteousness by means of the Mosaic Law, which they claimed included the oral traditions, since they believed that both the written Law and the oral traditions were given by God to Moses at Mount Sinai. _w 10/1/90_ 14a

## Thursday, January 30

_He who opposes the authority has taken a stand against the arrangement of God; those who have taken a stand against it will receive judgment to themselves.—Rom. 13:2._

National governments set down systems of laws, and most of these laws are good. Should this surprise us, in view of the fact that 'the whole world is lying in the power of the wicked one'? (1 John 5:19) No. Jehovah gave our original father, Adam, a conscience, and this innate sense of right and wrong is reflected in many ways in human laws. (Rom. 2:13-16) Most governments would say that the purpose of their laws is similar: to promote the welfare of citizens and good order in society. Hence, they penalize antisocial acts, such as murder and theft, and lay down regulations, such as speed limits and parking laws. Any who deliberately break their laws take a stand against the authority and 'will receive judgment to themselves.' Judgment from whom? Not necessarily from God. If anyone acts unlawfully, the superior authority has the right to punish him. _w 11/1/90_ 3, 6, 7a

## Friday, January 31

_Now, however, there remain faith, hope, love, these three; but the greatest of these is love.—1 Cor. 13:13._

Here the word for love is _a-ga'pe_, the same one that the apostle John used when he said: 'God is love.' (1 John 4:8, 16) This is a love guided or governed by principle. It may or may not include affection and fondness, but it is an unselfish emotion or feeling concerned with doing good to others regardless of

## Saturday, February 1

_The kingdom of the world did become the kingdom of our Lord \[Jehovah] and of his Christ, and he \[Jehovah] will rule as king forever and ever.—Rev. 11:15._

How did Jehovah's Witnesses come to understand the length of the seven times? The Bible reveals that 'a time and times and half a time,' or three and a half times, equals 1,260 days. (Rev. 12:6, 14) Therefore, double that number, or seven times, would amount to 2,520 days. On the basis of the prophetic guide of 'a day for a year,' the seven times would equal 2,520 years. (Num. 14:34; Ezek. 4:6) By this calculation, the Gentile Times, which started in October 607 B.C.E., ended 2,520 years later in October 1914. In October 1914, Jehovah God set his beloved Son, the Lord Jesus Christ, on the throne in the heavenly Kingdom. At last, the Revelation vision of the Christian apostle John started to become a reality, and the above announcement could be made. What glorious news this is and what reason for the greatest happiness on the part of all joint heirs and subjects of that Kingdom!—Rev. 11:17. _w 10/15/90_ 16, 17a

the merits of the recipient or of any benefits accruing to the giver. Love of this kind caused God to give the dearest treasure of his heart, his only-begotten Son, Jesus Christ, 'in order that everyone exercising faith in him might not be destroyed but have everlasting life.' (John 3:16) As Paul so well reminds us: 'God recommends his own love to us in that, while we were yet sinners, Christ died for us.' (Rom. 5:7, 8) Yes, _a-ga'pe_ does good to others regardless of their status in life or the cost to the one expressing the love. _w 11/15/90_ 7

## Sunday, February 2

_Let us . . . look intently at the Chief Agent and Perfecter of our faith, Jesus.—Heb. 12:1, 2._

Unselfishly responding to God's love, Jesus Christ always directed attention to his God and Father. When someone addressed Jesus as 'Good Teacher,' he objected and said: 'Nobody is good, except one, God.' (Luke 18:18, 19) Jesus repeatedly stressed that he could do nothing of his own initiative. He never missed an opportunity to magnify his Father's name, and he most fittingly began his model prayer with the petition: 'Let your name be sanctified.' He prayed: 'Father, glorify your name.' And shortly before he died, Christ said to his Father: 'I have glorified you on the earth, having finished the work you have given me to do.' (Matt. 6:9; John 12:28; 17:4) Surely, in response to God's love, we should be like Jesus and seek to glorify Jehovah, praying for the sanctification of His holy name. Another way in which Jesus responded unselfishly to his Father's love was that he loved righteousness and hated wickedness.—Heb. 1:9. _w 12/1/90_ 12, 13

## Monday, February 3

_Honor Jehovah with your valuable things and with the first-fruits of all your produce._
—Prov. 3:9.

One way we can honor Jehovah God is by making monetary contributions to the worldwide preaching work that he has authorized. The privilege of making such contributions is an opportunity to honor Jehovah God that no one should overlook. We can also honor Jehovah God in our prayers, praising and thanking him. (1 Chron. 29:10-13) In fact, because we come to him in humility and with deep respect, our very coming to God in prayer is according him honor. Today many people, youths in particular, honor those they admire by imitating them—by talking like them and by acting like them. Often the persons they imitate are sports heroes or stars in the world of entertainment. But, as Christians, we should honor Jehovah God by endeavoring to imitate him. "Become imitators of God, as beloved children." (Eph. 5:1) Yes, by striving to imitate Jehovah, we honor him. _w 2/1/91_ 19, 20

## Tuesday, February 4

_He who opposes the authority has taken a stand against the arrangement of God; those who have taken a stand against it will receive judgment to themselves._—Rom. 13:2.

In the first century, Jesus said of his followers: "They are no part of the world, just as I am no part of the world." (John 17:14) Any so-called Christian who promotes revolution is very much a part of the world. He would do well to heed the above warning. Chris-

tians are ambassadors or envoys of God and of Christ. As such, they remain neutral in this world's conflicts. Even though some political systems allow more freedom than others, Christians do not promote or rank one system above another. They know that all systems are imperfect. It is "the arrangement of God" that these continue to exist until his Kingdom takes over. (Dan. 2:44) Hence, Christians remain peacefully subject to the superior authorities while promoting the eternal welfare of others by preaching the good news of the Kingdom. _w 11/1/90_ 3, 5a

## Wednesday, February 5

_Then God said once more to Moses: "This is what you are to say to the sons of Israel, 'Jehovah the God of your forefathers . . . has sent me to you.' This is my name to time indefinite, and this is the memorial of me to generation after generation.'_—Ex. 3:15.

We recall that when Moses was sent by the Most High God to the people of Israel enslaved in the land of Egypt, the question of who sent him raised itself in the minds of the people to whom Moses was sent. Moses anticipated that the suffering Jewish people would want to know the name of the God whom he represented. In this regard we read as above. When this information was emphasized to them, the Israelites must have felt very encouraged. Their deliverance was assured by the only true God, Jehovah. And how encouraging it must have been to have the prospect of getting acquainted with God when he would demonstrate the meaning of his personal name—not haughtily isolating himself! _w 12/15/90_ 5, 6a

## Thursday, February 6

_Foxes have dens and birds of heaven have roosts, but the Son of man has nowhere to lay down his head._—Matt. 8:20.

One way in which Jesus Christ responded unselfishly to God's love was by sacrificing himself in behalf of others, down to an ignominious and painful death on a torture stake. (Phil. 2:5-8) Jesus ministered to the physical needs of people by miraculously feeding multitudes and performing many cures. (Matt. 14:14-22; 15:32-39) He always put the interests of others ahead of his own. That is why he could say the above. Jesus was sensitive to the operation of God's spirit as it flowed from him in performing miraculous cures. But he never tried to profit materially from the use of such supernatural power, as when a woman subject to a flow of blood for 12 years touched his outer garment in faith and was made well. (Mark 5:25-34) Moreover, Jesus never used supernatural powers in his own behalf.—Compare Matthew 4:2-4. _w 12/1/90_ 15

## Friday, February 7

_It is quite true to say that a man who sets his heart on leadership has laudable ambition._
—1 Tim. 3:1, "Phillips."

In what way does a man who sets his heart on being a Christian overseer have a "laudable ambition"? Well, ambition is an ardent desire to achieve a particular objective. If a man humbly reaches out for an office of overseer because he wants to serve others, his service is rendered with upright motives and can result in spiritual blessings. But he needs to guard

his heart. (Prov. 4:23) A Christian overseer who cares for his duties with the right motive will not pursue selfish ambitions. He will consider this fine work of Christian oversight a God-given privilege and will shepherd God's flock "not . . . for love of dishonest gain, but eagerly; neither as lording it over those who are God's inheritance, but becoming \[an example] to the flock." (1 Pet. 5:2, 3) Yes, overseers should guard against developing pride and seeking to wield power abusively. _w 9/1/90_ 2, 3, 5

## Saturday, February 8

_I will exalt you, O my God the King, and I will bless your name to time indefinite, even forever. All day long I will bless you, and I will praise your name to time indefinite, even forever._—Ps. 145:1, 2.

A fine way to honor Jehovah is by using our lips to witness about him. If we are truly concerned with bringing honor to him, then we will be conscientious in spreading the good news of the Kingdom. We can do this by going from house to house and by whatever other methods are available to us, not overlooking opportunities to witness informally. (John 4:6-26; Acts 5:42; 20:20) In addition, we have opportunities to honor our God with our voices at our congregation meetings, both by making comments and by joining in heartfelt singing of our Kingdom songs. (Heb. 2:12; 10:24, 25) In our daily conversation, we can also honor Jehovah God with our lips. With a little effort, we can maneuver the conversation into upbuilding spiritual channels, and this will result in bringing honor to Jehovah God. _w 2/1/91_ 16

## Sunday, February 9

_The spirit and the bride keep on saying: 'Come!' And let anyone hearing say: 'Come!'_

Why are God and the figurative Bridegroom not mentioned here? First, note that the verse does not state under whose direction the spirit acts. Yet, the reference to spirit directs our attention to Jehovah God himself. The Father is not pushed out of the picture, since he is the very Source of holy spirit. Second, the Son cooperates fully with his Father, as he himself says: 'The Son cannot do a single thing of his own initiative, but only what he beholds the Father doing.' (John 5:19) Moreover, while this invitation is an inspired utterance ultimately originating with Jehovah God, humans can receive divine direction by means of Jesus Christ, 'the Word.' (John 1:1) Appropriately, then, we associate Christ, the Bridegroom, with this invitation. Yes, both Jehovah God and Jesus Christ join with 'the bride' by means of the holy spirit in saying, 'Come!' _w 12/15/90_ 10

## Monday, February 10

_It is God's minister to you for your good. But if you are doing what is bad, be in fear: for it is not without purpose that it bears the sword; for it is God's minister, an avenger to express wrath upon the one practicing what is bad._—Rom. 13:4.

The term 'good deed' refers to acts in obedience to the laws of the superior authorities. (Rom. 13:3) In addition, our preaching work, which is commanded by God, not man, is not a bad deed—a point that the political authorities

should recognize. It is a public service that uplifts the moral caliber of those who respond. Therefore, it is our hope that the superior authorities will protect our right to preach to others. Paul appealed to the authorities in order to establish legally the preaching of the good news. (Acts 16:35-40; 25:8-12; Phil. 1:7) In recent years, Jehovah's Witnesses have similarly sought and obtained legal recognition of their work in Hungary, Poland, Romania, Benin, Myanmar (Burma), and former East Germany. Speaking of the secular authority, Paul continues as noted above. _w 11/1/90_ 11, 12a

## Tuesday, February 11

_Do I not hate those who are intensely hating you, O Jehovah, and do I not feel a loathing for those revolting against you?_

Loyalty to Jehovah will motivate us to take the wise course of hating what he hates. Further, no matter how pleasurable or exciting the breaking of one of God's laws may seem to be, we must keep telling ourselves that doing so is just not worth it. (Gal. 6:7, 8) The human heart is treacherous, devious, deceitful, even as we are reminded at Jeremiah 17:9. The Christian heart likes good, beautiful, pure things. But at times sinful tendencies incline it to desire also what is bad. Like the hearts of the Israelites who worshiped Jehovah and yet kept their idolatrous high places, so our own heart may be selfish and tricky. Our imperfect heart may try to find excuses to put us in the way of temptation. It may try to minimize the seriousness of the wrongdoing with which we are tempted. Or our heart may try to convince us that any punishment will merely be temporary. _w 12/1/90_ 6a

## Wednesday, February 12

_Everyone that has left \[all] for the sake of my name will receive many times more and will inherit everlasting life._

We have a striking example of this in the apostle Paul, who enjoyed many blessings, as recorded especially by Luke in the book of Acts. True love for God and for his fellowman caused Paul to give up his career as an honored Pharisee. Think, too, of what Paul endured in the way of blows, near-deaths, dangers, and privations—all because of true love for God and His sacred service. (2 Cor. 11:23-27) And how Jehovah rewarded Paul! Think of how fruitful Paul's ministry was. He was able to establish one Christian congregation after another. And what miracles God empowered him to perform! (Acts 19:11, 12) Paul was also privileged to receive supernatural visions and to write 14 letters that are now part of the Christian Greek Scriptures. To crown all of this, the prize of immortality in the heavens was given to him. _w 11/15/90_ 8, 9a

## Thursday, February 13

_I—I am the One that is wiping out your transgressions for my own sake, and your sins I shall not remember._—Isa. 43:25.

Satan cast a dark shadow across God's good name by accusing Jehovah of being a liar and a cruel dictator who deprived his creatures of knowledge and freedom. (Gen. 3:1-5) Furthermore, by seemingly thwarting God's purpose to fill the earth with righteous humans, Satan made God appear to be a failure. (Gen. 1:28; Isa. 55:10, 11) Satan also emboldened

ened himself to slander God's loyal servants, charging that they served Him only out of selfish motives. If these challenges were left unanswered, confidence in and support for God's rulership would finally be eroded. (Prov. 14:28) If law and order deteriorated, would havoc not reign throughout the universe? God thus owed it to himself and to his righteous ways to vindicate his sovereignty. He owed it to his faithful servants to allow them to demonstrate their unbreakable loyalty to him. _w 2/15/91_ 4, 5

## Friday, February 14

_An answer, when mild, turns away rage, but a word causing pain makes anger to come up._

The one pursuing peace is especially careful in his use of the tongue. James warns: 'The tongue is a little member and yet makes great brags. Look! How little a fire it takes to set so great a woodland on fire!' (Jas. 3:5) Mischievous gossip, criticism of others behind their back, unkind and harsh words, murmuring and complaining—all of these are works of the flesh that disturb the peace of God's people. True, James said: 'The tongue, not one of mankind can get it tamed.' (Jas. 3:8) Even mature Christians sometimes say things they sincerely regret afterward. All of us hope that others will forgive us such mistakes just as we forgive them. (Matt. 6:12) Sometimes a sharp burst of anger may produce hurtful words. Then, a peacemaker will remember the above-stated principle. Often, he will just have to take a deep breath and refuse to respond to angry words with more angry words. _w 3/1/91_ 17, 18a

## Saturday, February 15

_The spirit and the bride keep on saying: 'Come!' And let anyone hearing say: 'Come!' And let anyone thirsting come; let anyone that wishes take life's water free.—Rev. 22:17._

For decades this invitation to 'come!' has been going forth to people thirsting for 'life's water.' Even back in 1918, the bride class began preaching a message that particularly involved those who might live on the earth. It was the public talk entitled 'Millions Now Living May Never Die.' This offered a hope that many would survive Armageddon and thereafter gain eternal life on the Paradise earth under God's Messianic Kingdom. But that message did not definitely show the way to this survival privilege, except by righteousness in general. To reach more people with the invitation, 'Come!' in 1922 the message went out to all those who were interested in serving God: 'Advertise the King and Kingdom.' It highlighted the responsibility of the bride class to extend the invitation, 'Come!' _w 12/15/90_ 11, 12

## Sunday, February 16

_If anyone wants to come after me, let him disown himself and pick up his torture stake and continually follow me.—Matt. 16:24._

How can we honor the Son? We do so by exercising faith in his ransom sacrifice, and we prove that faith by taking the necessary steps of repentance, conversion, dedication, and baptism. By coming to Jehovah in prayer in Jesus' name, we honor Jesus. We further honor him when we heed his above words. We honor Jesus Christ

when we heed his instructions to keep seeking first God's Kingdom and his righteousness, and we honor him when we heed his command to share in the disciple-making work. Again, we honor Jesus when we manifest the brotherly love that he said would identify all his true followers. (Matt. 6:33; 28:19, 20; John 13:34, 35) Further, we bring honor to the Son by taking upon ourselves his name, calling ourselves Christians, and then by living up to that name by our fine conduct and by our preaching. _w 2/1/91_ 17, 18a

## Monday, February 17

_For those ruling are an object of fear, not to the good deed, but to the bad. Do you, then, want to have no fear of the authority? Keep doing good, and you will have praise from it.—Rom. 13:3._

Jehovah's Witnesses have a good reputation for not opposing human authorities. If it happens that an individual in the congregation does break the law, the congregation will not help him evade lawful punishment. If anyone steals, murders, libels, cheats on his taxes, rapes, defrauds, uses illegal drugs, or in any other way opposes lawful authority, he will face severe discipline from the congregation—and he should not feel persecuted when he is punished by the secular authority. Paul in his discussion of the superior authorities says the above. It is not loyal Christians who should fear punishment from the authority but wrongdoers, those who commit 'bad deeds,' criminal acts. When threatened by such lawless elements, Jehovah's Witnesses may properly accept from the authority police or military protection. _w 11/1/90_ 8, 9a

## Tuesday, February 18

_When the living creatures went, the wheels would go beside them, and when the living creatures were lifted up from the earth, the wheels would be lifted up.—Ezek. 1:19._

The submissive angels move in concert as part of God's heavenly organization while assisting Jehovah's earthly servants in fulfilling their commission to declare divine judgment warnings. If we desire the continued protection and guidance of these mighty angelic servants of God, we too must move in concert and keep pace with the symbolic wheelwork. Moreover, as part of Jehovah's visible organization moving in parallel with his celestial chariot, we must be sensitive to the leadings of God's spirit. (Compare Philippians 2:13.) If we are Jehovah's Witnesses, we must move in the same direction as the celestial chariot. We surely must not work at cross-purposes with it. When given direction in the way we should go, we should follow it. Thus, the congregation is not divided.—1 Cor. 1:10. _w 3/15/91_ 18

## Wednesday, February 19

_O let people give thanks to Jehovah for his loving-kindness and for his wonderful works to the sons of men.—Ps. 107:8._

Jehovah has truly been bounteous in manifesting love for his creatures. The Scriptures give us fine examples of those who have responded unselfishly to manifestations of God's love. Most fittingly, the inspired psalmist exclaimed as noted above. Dare we accept God's undeserved kindness and miss its purpose? Never may

that happen! (2 Cor. 6:1) So let each of us individually ask ourselves: 'Do I really appreciate expressions of God's love that I have already enjoyed and confidently hope to enjoy further in the future? Are they motivating me to love Jehovah with all my heart, soul, mind, and strength? Am I truly God-oriented? Do I love righteousness and hate lawlessness? Am I showing brotherly love? And how closely am I trying to walk in Jesus' footsteps as regards my ministry?' Truly, there are many ways to show our sincere gratitude for all the manifestations of God's love that we have experienced. _w 12/1/90_ 16, 17a

## Thursday, February 20

_If any one of you is lacking in wisdom, let him keep on asking God, for he gives generously to all.—Jas. 1:5._

Why might a mature Christian not be reaching out? He may be a spiritual man but feel inadequate. Of course, we should have a modest view of ourselves, being aware of our limitations. Rather than presumptuously thinking we are the most qualified for a certain responsibility, it is good to remember that 'wisdom is with the modest ones.' (Prov. 11:2) But we should also realize that if God grants us a privilege of service, he will also supply the strength needed to carry it out. As Paul said: 'For all things I have the strength by virtue of him who imparts power to me.' (Phil. 4:13) A Christian may not reach out because he feels that he lacks sufficient wisdom to give counsel. Well, perhaps he can gain wisdom by being a more diligent student of God's Word, and certainly he should pray for wisdom, as noted above. _w 9/1/90_ 10, 11

---

<sup>**Page 14**</sup>

[Open Page 14 of PDF](https://pdfs-noindex.themindscapeishere.workers.dev/1992_es_E.pdf#page=14)

## Friday, February 21

_The one holding back his rod is hating his son, but the one loving him is he that does look for him with discipline._

—Prov. 13:24.

One indication that we are living in “the last days” with their “critical times hard to deal with” is a lack of “natural affection.” (2 Tim. 3:1, 3) Because family love is lacking, certain youths run away from home, and some grown children neglect their aged parents. How this contrasts to the advice given at Proverbs 23:22: “Listen to your father who caused your birth, and do not despise your mother just because she has grown old.” The lack of natural affection is also seen in the prevalence of child abuse—some parents beating their children so severely that they require hospitalization. Lack of parental love is evident, too, in the failure of many parents to discipline their children. Letting children have their own way is not an evidence of love but amounts to following the line of least resistance. A father who truly loves his children will discipline them when necessary.—Heb. 12:5-11. w 11/15/90 5

## Saturday, February 22

_Do not be anxious over anything, but in everything by prayer and supplication along with thanksgiving let your petitions be made known to God; and the peace of God that excels all thought will guard your hearts and your mental powers by means of Christ Jesus._

—Phil. 4:6, 7.

A child nestled in a loving father’s arms has a feeling of peace, an unquestioning certainty that he is watched over by someone

who cares for him. Paul encouraged the Philippians as above. One result of man’s loss of peace with God was hatred and discord. For first-century Christians, finding peace with God resulted in the very opposite: peace and unity among themselves, what Paul called “the uniting bond of peace.” (Eph. 4:3) They “thought in agreement and lived peaceably, and the God of love and peace was with them.” Moreover, they preached “the good news of peace,” which was, essentially, the good news of salvation for “friends of peace,” those who respond to the good news.—2 Cor. 13:11; Acts 10:36; Luke 10:5, 6. w 3/1/91 15, 16

## Sunday, February 23

_The spirit and the bride keep on saying: “Come!” And let anyone hearing say: “Come!”_

—Rev. 22:17.

As early as 1932, _The Watchtower_ pointed out the responsibility of the “other sheep,” in their turn, to say, “Come!” (John 10:16) In its issue of August 1, page 232, paragraph 29 stated: “Jehovah’s witnesses now have the zeal like to that of Jehu and they should encourage the Jehonadab class \[other sheep] to come along with them and to take some part in proclaiming to others that the kingdom of God is at hand.” Then, after quoting Revelation 22:17, the paragraph said: “Let the anointed encourage all who will to take part in telling the good news of the kingdom. They do not have to be anointed of the Lord in order to declare the Lord’s message. It is a great comfort to Jehovah’s witnesses to now know that they are permitted to carry the waters of life to a class of people that may be taken through Armageddon and given everlasting life on the earth by reason of the bounteous goodness of Jehovah.” w 12/15/90 13

## Monday, February 24

_In showing honor to one another take the lead._—Rom. 12:10.

There is the responsibility everyone has to show honor within the Christian congregation, as we are counseled above. Jesus indicated in one of his illustrations that it is proper to accept honor. He said that when we are invited to a feast, we should take the lowest place, for then our host will ask us to take a higher seat, and we will have honor in front of all our fellow guests. (Luke 14:10) Now, since all of us appreciate being accorded honor, should we not have empathy and accord honor to one another? How can we do this? Expressions of appreciation for a task well done are tantamount to bestowing honor. So we can honor one another by giving commendation, perhaps for a talk or comment someone presents in the congregation. In addition, we can honor one another by girding ourselves with lowliness of mind toward our Christian brothers and sisters. We thus demonstrate that we value them as honorable fellow servants of Jehovah God. w 2/1/91 12, 13b

## Tuesday, February 25

_We must obey God as ruler rather than men._—Acts 5:29.

Was it consistent for Paul to appeal to the Roman imperial authority when Jesus had referred to Satan as the real “ruler of the world” and Paul himself had called Satan “the god of this system of things”? (John 14:30; 2 Cor. 4:4; Acts 25:11, 12) Or did the Roman authority occupy some “relative position” that made it appropriate for Paul to look to that

authority for protection of his rights? Indeed, do the earlier words of the apostles, noted above, allow for Christian obedience to human rulers whenever disobedience to God is not involved? Paul helps us to answer these questions in his letter to the Romans, where he reveals a mature view of human rulership. At Romans 13:1-7, Paul makes plain the part a Christian’s conscience must play in balancing complete obedience to the Supreme Authority, Jehovah God, with relative obedience to “the superior authorities.” w 11/1/90 2, 3

## Wednesday, February 26

_Jehovah is giving support to all who are falling, and is raising up all who are bowed down._

—Ps. 145:14.

Since the days of Abel, Jehovah has supported His worshippers. Left to ourselves, we would fall many times under our burdens. We lack sufficient strength to bear all of life’s woes and the persecution that comes our way as God’s people, but Jehovah upholds us. The form of the Hebrew verb used here indicates that God continuously “gives us support.” It is comforting to know that “Jehovah is raising up all who are bowed down” by various trials. He cheers the despondent among us, comforts our grieving ones, and helps us to speak his word boldly when we are persecuted. (Acts 4:29-31) Never does he allow our burdens to crush us if only we accept his help. (Ps. 55:22) So, like the “daughter of Abraham” who was “bent double” but was physically cured by Jesus, we should “glorify God” when he lovingly raises us up spiritually.—Luke 13:10-17. w 9/15/90 10, 11a

## Thursday, February 27

\[He was] loyal, guileless, undefiled, separated from the sinners.—Heb. 7:26.

One way in which an unselfish response to God's love motivated Jesus was by causing him to love righteousness and hate wickedness, as foretold at Psalm 45:7. (Heb. 1:9) Jesus challenged his malicious opposers to convict him of sin, but they could not do so. (John 8:46) On two occasions, his hatred of wickedness caused him to cleanse the temple of greedy religionists. (Matt. 21:12, 13; John 2:13-17) And how scathingly Jesus denounced the hypocritical religious leaders, even telling them that they were of the Devil! (Matt. 6:2, 5, 16; 15:7-9; 23:13-32; John 8:44) Still another way in which Jehovah's love motivated Jesus can be seen in his dealings with his apostles and other disciples. How loving, patient, and long-suffering he was with them! They must have tried him sorely with their rivalry, arguing even down to the very night of his betrayal about who was the greatest.—Luke 22:24-27. w 12/1/90 13, 14

## Friday, February 28

God loved the world so much that he gave his only-begotten Son, in order that everyone exercising faith in him might not be destroyed but have everlasting life.—John 3:16.

Only Jehovah could provide the perfect "Lamb . . . that takes away the sin of the world." (John 1:29) God did not send some angel to rescue mankind. He made the supreme sacrifice of sending his only-begotten Son, "the one he was specially fond of." (Prov. 8:30) God's Son "emptied himself" of his heavenly nature. (Phil. 2:7) Jeho-

vah transferred the life-force and the personality pattern of his firstborn heavenly Son to the womb of a Jewish virgin named Mary. Holy spirit then 'overshadowed her,' guaranteeing that the child growing in her womb would be absolutely free of sin. (Luke 1:35) As a man, he would be called Jesus. But in a legal sense, he could be called 'the second Adam,' for he corresponded perfectly to Adam. (1 Cor. 15:45, 47) Jesus could thus offer himself up in sacrifice as "an unblemished and spotless lamb," a ransom for sinful mankind.—1 Pet. 1:18, 19. w 2/15/91 15, 16

## Saturday, February 29

Everyone, then, that confesses union with me before men, I will also confess union with him before my Father who is in the heavens; but whoever disowns me before men, I will also disown him before my Father who is in the heavens.
—Matt. 10:32, 33.

The apostle Peter said that we should follow Jesus' steps closely. (1 Pet. 2:21) By thus imitating him in all our conduct, we also honor him. And certainly, when we annually celebrate the Memorial of Christ's death, we accord him special honor. (1 Cor. 11:23-26) What rewards does Jesus hold out to his disciples for following a course that honors him? From what he said at Mark 10:29, 30, it follows that if we make sacrifices for Jesus' sake, he will see to it that we are rewarded. Moreover, Jesus assures us as above. So just as the heavenly Father honors those who honor him, we can be confident that Jehovah's only-begotten Son will imitate his Father in this respect, even as the Son does in other matters. w 2/1/91 18-20a

## Sunday, March 1

"Whom shall I send, and who will go for us?" And I proceeded to say: "Here I am! Send me."
—Isa. 6:8.

When the Isaiah class in modern times began to hear the voice of Jehovah asking: "Whom shall I send, and who will go for us?" they gladly responded: "Here I am! Send me." It was not easy for all of them to get started in the public ministry, but they wanted to be used by God as a people for his name. His spirit strengthened them. Their numbers grew. In time it became evident that their preaching was yielding unexpected results. Through them, Jehovah was helping another group to learn the pure language. (Isa. 55:5) These did not share the hope of heavenly life, but they counted it a privilege to be companions of the remnant of Kingdom heirs and to serve shoulder to shoulder with them as proclaimers of God's Kingdom. Progressively, these have come out of "all nations and tribes and peoples and tongues" until they now make up "a great crowd" numbering into the millions.—Rev. 7:9. w 4/1/91 15, 16a

## Monday, March 2

The lost one I shall search for, and the dispersed one I shall bring back.—Ezek. 34:16.

May the elders themselves take any initiative in approaching a disfellowshipped person? Yes. The Bible shows that mercy is expressed not simply by a negative holding back of punishment but often by positive acts. We have Jehovah's example. Before he sent his unfaithful people into exile, he prophetically held out the pros-

pect of their returning. (Isa. 44:21, 22) Then, during the exile, Jehovah took further steps, acting in a positive way. He sent prophets, his representatives, to invite Israel to 'seek him and find him.' (Jer. 29:1, 10-14) At Ezekiel 34:16, he likened himself to a shepherd and the people of the nation of Israel to lost sheep. At Jeremiah 31:10, Jehovah also used the imagery of his being a shepherd of the Israelites. He showed himself as a shepherd searching for the lost ones. Note that even while the people in general were unrepentant and exiled, God initiated efforts to seek their return. w 4/15/91 7, 8a

## Tuesday, March 3

Pay back . . . Caesar's things to Caesar, but God's things to God.—Matt. 22:21.

Since Jehovah's Witnesses are subject to human authority, why did Witnesses in Hitler's Germany suffer as they did? Because our subjection is relative, and the authority does not always recognize that there are Biblically set limits to what it can demand. If the authority demands something that offends a trained Christian conscience, it is going beyond its God-given limit. Jesus indicated this when he said the above. When Caesar demands what belongs to God, we must acknowledge that God has the prior claim. Is this position subversive or treacherous? Not at all. It is, in fact, an extension of a principle recognized by most civilized nations. The claim that a person committing atrocities is not accountable if he is following the orders of a superior authority has been made a number of times, but the claim has usually been rejected. w 11/1/90 10, 11b
